# Jarvis Agent — StartupNews.fyi Project Knowledge Base

> Auto-maintained by Jarvis. Updated on every interaction.

---

## Project Identity

- **Name:** StartupNews.fyi
- **Repo name:** zox-nextjs
- **Framework:** Next.js 16.1.6 (App Router)
- **Language:** TypeScript 5, React 19
- **Styling:** Tailwind CSS v3.4.17 (active — configured in interaction #7)
- **Package manager:** npm

---

## Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology |
|---|---|
| Frontend | Next.js App Router, React 19, Tailwind CSS v3.4.17 |
| Rich Text Editor | TipTap v3 |
| Database | MariaDB (via `mariadb` npm package) |
| Cache | Redis v5 |
| Storage | AWS S3 (`startupnews-media-2026`, us-east-1) |
| Auth | JWT (`jsonwebtoken`) + bcrypt |
| Email | Nodemailer + SMTP |
| Cron | `node-cron` v4 |
| RSS | `rss-parser` |
| Scraping | `cheerio` |
| Deployment | PM2 (`ecosystem.config.js`), Docker (`docker-compose.yml`) |

---

## Project Structure

```
src/
  app/              # Next.js App Router pages
    (admin)/        # Admin panel (auth-gated)
    about/          # About page
    api/            # API routes
    [...slug]/      # Dynamic post/category pages
  components/       # Shared UI components
  lib/              # Core utilities (data, auth, config, smtp)
  modules/          # Feature modules (posts, categories, events, banners, rss-feeds, users, public-users)
  shared/           # Cross-cutting concerns (cache, database, locks, middleware, utils)
  queue/            # In-memory job queue
  workers/          # RSS worker
  proxy.ts          # Next.js proxy/middleware: /post/* 410 handling, X-Robots-Tag on article paths
cron/               # Cron job definitions (RSS feed scheduler)
scripts/            # DB migrations, seed, import, export, backfill scripts
public/             # Static assets (no default next/vercel starter SVGs)
docs/               # Documentation (this agent lives here conceptually)
```

## About Us Page (`/about-us`)

- **Route:** `/about-us` (distinct from the older `/about` above — this is the canonical one)
- **Files:** `src/app/about-us/page.tsx` (client component, pink/cream editorial-magazine layout: hero, stat panel, video-card row + dot carousel, 6-icon pillar grid, "What We Do" CTA, footer bar), `src/app/about-us/layout.tsx`
- **Isolated Tailwind v4 build, fully automatic, one shared CSS file for both isolated-Tailwind routes (interaction #419)** — no manual regeneration step, no per-route CSS file:
  - `src/app/isolated-tailwind.css` (one level up from this route, shared with `/advertise-with-us`) is both the Tailwind *source* (has `@import`/`@theme` directives for **both** routes) AND the exact file `layout.tsx` imports (`import "../isolated-tailwind.css"`). There is no separate compiled-output file and no CLI command to remember — `@tailwindcss/postcss` (enabled project-wide in `postcss.config.mjs`) compiles it automatically as part of Next's normal webpack CSS pipeline, on every `npm run dev` and `npm run build`, exactly like a standard Tailwind project. Just edit `page.tsx` classNames and run the normal build/dev command. Webpack correctly dedupes the shared import into a single compiled chunk served to both routes (confirmed: one ~13KB chunk containing both `au-*` and `adv-*` utilities, not two separate ones).
  - `postcss.config.mjs` has `@tailwindcss/postcss` listed **before** `postcss-import` (order matters — `postcss-import` doesn't understand Tailwind's `@import "tailwindcss/theme.css" source(none)` syntax and will mis-resolve it as a plain file import if it runs first; this was the actual cause of the old "Module not found" errors that originally justified the manual-CLI workaround). Verified this doesn't affect `globals.css`/`style.css`/`media-queries.css` at all — `@tailwindcss/postcss` only transforms files that actually contain Tailwind directives; everything else passes through byte-for-byte unchanged (confirmed via identical compiled-output hash before/after, both when this was first set up in #418 and again after merging into one shared file in #419).
  - `isolated-tailwind.css` still needs `source(none)` on both `@import`s + one explicit `@source` line **per route** (`./about-us/page.tsx` and `./advertise-with-us/page.tsx`, relative to the shared file's own location) — remove any of these and Tailwind falls back to scanning the whole repo for candidate classes, which once picked up bracket-syntax example text in this very `agent.md` and generated broken CSS that crashed the build (interaction #410). If a third route ever wants to join this shared Tailwind setup, add another `@source` line there rather than creating a new file.
  - **Cascade-layer gotcha (root-caused in interaction #412) still applies:** the `@import` lines must **not** use `layer(theme)`/`layer(utilities)` — that would wrap the output in named `@layer` blocks, which lose every cascade battle against the site's unlayered legacy reset. Keep the imports plain.
  - Still runs under PM2 in **production mode** (`next start`): after any `page.tsx` className change, just `npm run build` (no separate CSS-compile step anymore) then `pm2 restart dev-startupgpt`.
- **Reference source of truth:** a hand-designed static `about-us (1).html` prototype the owner approved — theme tokens (`--color-au-pink`, `--color-au-pink-deep`, `--color-au-ink`, `--color-au-muted`, `--color-au-line`, `--color-au-bg`, `--color-au-panel`, `--color-au-fade`) live in `isolated-tailwind.css`'s `@theme` block, named after that prototype's CSS custom properties.

## Advertise With Us Page (`/advertise-with-us`)

- **Route:** `/advertise-with-us` — pre-existing, real, live route with a working lead-capture form; this was a visual redesign, not a new build.
- **Files:** `src/app/advertise-with-us/page.tsx` (client component: hero, "reach the audience" split, 8-stat grid, "ways to work with us" numbered list, dark 4-card "why choose us" section, enquiry form), `layout.tsx` (imports `../isolated-tailwind.css`, shared with about-us — see that section above for the full build-pipeline rationale).
- **Same isolated Tailwind v4 build pattern as `/about-us`, one shared CSS file for both routes (interaction #419)**. No CLI command, no separate compiled-output file, no per-route CSS file. After any `page.tsx` className change, just `npm run build` → `pm2 restart dev-startupgpt`.
- **Theme tokens** (in `src/app/isolated-tailwind.css`'s `@theme` block, alongside about-us's `au-*` tokens): `--color-adv-red` `#E8232A`, `--color-adv-red-deep` `#B8151B`, `--color-adv-ink` `#0F1115`, `--color-adv-muted` `#6B7280`, `--color-adv-muted-2` `#9AA1A9`, `--color-adv-line` `#E9EBEE`, `--color-adv-line-2` `#DFE3E7`, `--color-adv-panel` `#F6F7F8`.
- **Form is real, not a stub**: posts to the pre-existing `src/app/api/advertise/route.ts` (SMTP-backed, sends to `office@startupnews.fyi`), preserving its exact expected payload (`firstName`, `companyName`, `email`, `phone`, `budgetRate`, `campaignGoal`, `objective`, `turnstileToken`) and its Cloudflare Turnstile CAPTCHA requirement (`@marsidev/react-turnstile`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` env var) — the route 400s without a valid `turnstileToken`, so the widget must stay even though the design reference itself has no CAPTCHA.
- **Placeholder images** (interaction #414): the reference mockup's hero/"ways to work" images were a soon-to-expire signed S3 URL and an unsigned S3 URL respectively — neither usable long-term, so both were swapped for Unsplash stock photos (`photo-1600880292203-757bb62b4baf`, `photo-1600880292089-90a7e086ee0c`). **Swap these for real brand assets when available.**
- Uses the shared `Header`/`Footer` components (wired via `ConditionalLayout.tsx`) — the reference's own hardcoded top-bar/nav/footer markup was intentionally not reproduced.

## Tailwind CSS Migration Status

### Styling Rule Going Forward

- **Final target: Tailwind-only codebase, including removal of existing CSS.**
- Do **not** add new custom CSS, CSS Modules, `<style jsx>`, or static inline `style={{}}` props.
- Use Tailwind utilities, arbitrary values, responsive variants, `group`/`peer` states, and `tailwind.config.js` extensions for animations/design tokens.
- Treat all remaining custom/legacy CSS as migration debt to remove, not as a permanent dependency.
- When replacing existing CSS, migrate the behavior into TSX Tailwind classes instead of creating new stylesheet rules.
- Existing CSS may remain only temporarily during incremental migration to avoid breaking pages.

**ALL CONTROLLABLE SURFACES USE TAILWIND IN TSX — LEGACY THEME CSS STILL LOADED (interaction #46 clarified)**
Public pages, public components, and admin UI are authored with **Tailwind utility classes** wherever we control the markup. The project **still loads legacy theme CSS** (`styles/style.css`, `styles/media-queries.css`) plus `globals.css` overrides because the homepage and article layouts depend on **`mvp-*`** and related theme classes until a full redesign replaces that layer. **`@tailwind base` is intentionally not used** — it breaks the theme float/sticky layout (see interaction #9). **Exceptions:** TipTap admin editor (`<style>` injections for editor chrome + pasted content), 410 HTML in `proxy.ts`, Font Awesome CDN `@import` in `globals.css`.

### Shared Tailwind Patterns Used

- Section headings: `text-2xl font-extrabold text-black mt-12 mb-5 uppercase tracking-[0.5px]`
- Pink accent divider: `w-[60px] h-1 bg-[#ee1761] mx-auto`
- Body article: `text-base leading-[1.8] text-[#333]`
- Lists: `pl-5 mb-10 list-disc space-y-2`
- Pink links: `text-[#ee1761]`
- Page wrapper: `w-full bg-white overflow-hidden min-h-screen`
- Content container: `max-w-[800px] mx-auto px-5 py-20`

## Hybrid Tailwind Rebuild — `/home/tech/StartupnewsNewCodebase` (2026-05-16)

**Goal:** Rebuild frontend UI entirely with Tailwind CSS v3.4.17, reusing backend/data/API/admin logic from the original project. No legacy CSS. Same visual UI.

### Configuration
- `tailwindcss` 3.4.17 + `autoprefixer` ^10.4.20 (devDependencies)
- `postcss.config.mjs`: postcss-import → tailwindcss → autoprefixer
- `tailwind.config.js`: content scans `src/app`, `src/components`, `src/modules`; custom `brand.pink` (#ee1761), `brand.dark` (#0a0a0a), `maxWidth.site` (1200px)
- `src/app/globals.css`: `@tailwind base/components/utilities` only, no legacy CSS imports

- `src/components/HomeDarkSection.tsx` — dark background, 1 large featured + 4 list items

## Admin Panel

### Admin Sidebar Menu Items
Dashboard, Posts, Events, Event Regions, Categories, Authors, Banners, RSS Feeds, **Registered Users** (`/admin/registered-users`)
(List above predates several later additions — see `src/components/admin/AdminSidebar.tsx` `menuItems` for the current full set, which also includes Partnership Tracker, Newsletter, **Sales Report**, Tools, Reports, Brand Stories, Database/Contacts, Users.)

### Sales Tracker (`/admin/sales-tracker`, renamed from Sales Report, see log #392) — DB-backed since log #409
- A standalone leads/CRM tracker (Add/Edit lead, screenshot/voice-note AI extraction, team members, filterable table, status/type breakdown + bar charts), embedded via `<iframe src="/api/admin-tools/sales-tracker/index.html">` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/sales-tracker/page.tsx`, served by `src/app/api/admin-tools/[tool]/[file]/route.ts` (whitelisted tool/file lookup — see `AdminIframeTool.tsx`).
- Source lives in `src/modules/admin-tools/sales-tracker/` (`index.html` + `styles.css` + `script.js`), not `public/` (moved at some point after the original 2026-08-08 add) — kept out of the app's own CSS/component tree by design, per the original user request.
- **Persistence (log #409):** was `localStorage`-only (not shared across team members, despite the tool's own "Shared across your team" subtitle) — migrated to real MySQL tables `sales_leads` + `sales_team_members` (`scripts/migrations/add-sales-tracker-tables.sql`). Backend: `src/modules/sales-tracker/` (domain/repository/service) + `src/app/api/admin/sales-tracker/{leads,leads/[id],team}/route.ts`, gated by `SALES_TRACKER_ROLES` (`admin` only, `src/shared/middleware/roles.ts`). Frontend `script.js` now calls these routes directly (`apiGetLeads`/`apiSaveLead`/`apiDeleteLead`/`apiDeleteAllLeads`/`apiGetTeam`/`apiAddTeamMember`/`apiRemoveTeamMember`) instead of `storageGet`/`storageSet`; all hardcoded `sampleLeads()`/`sampleTeam()` dummy data removed, table starts empty until real leads are entered.

### HR Tool ("Huey", `/admin/hr-tool`, display label "HR Management" per log #408) — DB-backed since log #409
- A full standalone HR system prototype (directory, onboarding e-sign flow, attendance/punch, leave, expenses, payroll, tickets/helpdesk, compliance calendar, letter templates, Rules & Org Structure, audit log) with its own internal role-play login (Employee ID, any password) layered on top of — and independent from — the site's real admin auth. Embedded the same way as Sales Tracker: `src/app/(admin)/admin/hr-tool/page.tsx` → `/api/admin-tools/hr-tool/index.html` → `src/modules/admin-tools/hr-tool/script.js` (~1960 lines, single `state` object driving synchronous re-renders).
- **Persistence (log #409):** previously **zero persistence** — a hardcoded `state` seed that reset on every page reload, explicitly commented `"In-memory data store (no backend — resets on reload)"`. Migrated to 19 real MySQL tables (`hr_teams`, `hr_designations`, `hr_expense_categories`, `hr_required_documents`, `hr_holidays`, `hr_employees`, `hr_onboarding`, `hr_attendance`, `hr_attendance_overrides`, `hr_punch_log`, `hr_regularizations`, `hr_leave_requests`, `hr_expenses`, `hr_tickets`, `hr_compliance_tasks`, `hr_payroll_runs`, `hr_templates`, `hr_rules` singleton, `hr_audit_log`) — see `scripts/migrations/add-hr-tool-tables.sql`.
- Backend: `src/modules/hr-tool/` (domain/types.ts covers every entity; `repository/hr-tool.repository.ts` + `repository/shared.ts`'s generic `replaceAllRows`/`findAllRows` helpers; `service/hr-tool.service.ts`) + `src/app/api/admin/hr-tool/*` routes (one `GET /bootstrap` that returns the whole initial state in one call, `PUT` bulk-replace routes for list entities, `POST` upsert routes for single-record entities like attendance/punch/payroll-runs/audit-log/templates/[name]), gated by `HR_TOOL_ROLES` (`admin` only).
- Frontend rewiring pattern: `state` starts as an empty skeleton, `loadAll()` fetches `/bootstrap` at boot before first render; every mutating function became `async` and now `await`s a matching `persist*()` helper (defined near the top of `script.js`) right after mutating local `state`, before re-rendering — e.g. `persistEmployees()` does `PUT /employees` with the full `state.employees` array. `logRuleChange()` (audit log) is fire-and-forget (`persistAuditEntry`, not awaited) since it's called from ~15 non-critical sites.
- **Dummy data removed, real setup kept:** all demo employees/onboarding/attendance/leave/expense/ticket records removed. Org structure (teams, designations, expense categories, required documents, holidays), letter templates (Offer Letter / Employment Agreement keep their real DOTFYI Media Ventures boilerplate; other 5 template types seeded empty), and policy `rules` defaults were kept as seed data — the tool's own `resetSampleData()` button already drew this exact line ("kept Teams, Org Structure, Rules, and Templates, since that's real setup, not sample data"). **One employee is seeded** (`E-105`, Divya Menon, HR Head) so there's a way to log in at all post-migration — team `manager` fields pointing at now-removed demo employees were nulled out.
- **Note (log #426):** the two paragraphs above describe the original iframe/`script.js` build; the tool was since rewritten to native React (`src/app/(admin)/admin/hr-tool/page.tsx` → `HrToolProvider`/`HrToolApp` in `src/components/admin/hr-tool/`, backed by the same `src/modules/hr-tool/` API) — this section hasn't been fully rewritten to match yet, treat file paths above as historical. As of log #426: the internal "Huey" Employee ID + password login screen (`views/Login.tsx`) is bypassed — opening HR Management always lands directly on the `Founder` HR-role (`HrToolContext.tsx`'s `resolveFounder()`), since the outer route is already `admin`-only. A new **Assigning IDs** People-tab (`views/AssigningIds.tsx`, Founder-only) lets the Founder issue a real Employee ID + password (bcrypt-verified, stored in the new `hr_employee_credentials` table — see `src/modules/hr-credentials/`) and optionally link it to an existing Publisher Admin / Event Admin `panel_admins` account, at which point that account's original email/password login is retired in favour of Employee ID + password (`PanelAdminsService.loginWithEmployeeId`, `/admin/login`'s new Email/Employee ID tab toggle). Full detail in log #426.
- **Note (log #429):** Publisher Admin/Event Admin now have their own Punch In/Punch Out card directly on their `/admin` dashboard (`src/components/admin/AttendanceWidget.tsx`), gated by a new route group `src/app/api/admin/attendance/` (`requireAnyRole(['event_admin','publisher_admin'])` — separate from `HR_TOOL_ROLES`). It resolves their identity via `HrCredentialsService.getByLinkedPanelAdminId()` (reverse lookup off the JWT's `panel_admins.id`) and writes into the same `hr_attendance`/`hr_punch_log` tables described above, so their punches show up directly in the Founder's Attendance view (`views/Attendance.tsx`, now also showing Employee ID + Role columns via `HrBootstrap.employeeCredentials`). Once-per-day is enforced server-side (calendar-day reset), unlike the original Employee self-service flow which only checked this client-side. Full detail in log #429.
- **Note (log #439):** DB audit found all 19 `add-hr-tool-tables.sql` tables present and seeded in live `zox_db`, but `hr_employee_credentials` (the `add-hr-employee-credentials-table.sql` migration underpinning Assigning IDs / employee login since log #426) had never actually been run — the code referencing it (`hr-credentials` module, `panel-admins` repository, `employee-auth` middleware) would have failed at query time. Migration run against live `zox_db`; table now exists (empty, 0 rows).
- **Note (log #443):** Rules & Org Structure's admin-configured Designations list and shift/grace-period settings are now wired into the rest of the product instead of sitting unused. `hr_employee_credentials.designation` is `VARCHAR(255)` (widened from a 3-value ENUM), and Assigning IDs' Designation dropdown pulls live from `hr_designations` (`HrToolService.getDesignations()`) instead of a hardcoded list. A new `HrToolService.getShiftSettings()` exposes just `{shiftStartTime, shiftEndTime, shiftGraceMinutes}` (not the rest of `hr_rules`) to the isolated Publisher/Event Admin and plain-employee attendance surfaces; the shared lateness calculator now lives at `src/modules/hr-tool/utils/lateness.ts` (framework-agnostic, re-exported by the HR Tool's own `utils.tsx`) so both `AttendanceWidget.tsx` (Publisher/Event Admin + plain-employee dashboards) and the Founder's own `views/Attendance.tsx` Today table show identical, grace-adjusted lateness figures for every punch.
- **Note (log #445):** `AttendanceWidget.tsx` (the shared Punch In/Out card on both the Publisher/Event Admin and plain-employee dashboards) is no longer a "punch buttons + last 14 days" list — it's now a selected-date table (Date/Status/Punch In/Punch Out, with the punch buttons living inside the table and only appearing when the selected day is today) paired with a big navigable month calendar whose day cells are color-coded green/orange/red by how that day's punch-in landed against the admin's shift start + grace period. This required adding real per-day lateness data that didn't exist before: `hr_attendance.in_minutes` (new column, `add-hr-attendance-in-minutes.sql`) — previously only `hr_punch_log` (single row per employee, overwritten daily) had the raw punch-in minutes, so past days had no way to answer "how late were they that day." `/api/admin/attendance/me` and `/api/employee/attendance/me` now take `?month=YYYY-MM` and return that month's day-by-day records (`calendar`) instead of a flat recent-history list.
- **Note (log #446):** Employees can now request attendance regularization themselves (previously only the Founder's own internal whole-array-replace flow existed, which an isolated employee/Publisher/Event Admin session could never safely call). From `AttendanceWidget.tsx`, selecting a date with a late/grace-period punch-in shows an "Apply for Regularization" button (reason required, capped by a new admin-set monthly quota, blocked once a request already exists for that date); submitted dates render light blue on the calendar regardless of approval outcome. New `hr_rules` columns: `regularizationMonthlyQuota`, `shortLeaveMaxHours`, `shortLeaveMonthlyQuota` (all editable in Rules & Org Structure). Employees never see the exact grace-period minute value anywhere in the Attendance module (admin-set, deliberately hidden so it can't be gamed) — but they DO see it, along with every other policy number, on the new dedicated **Rules & Policy** page (`PolicySummaryWidget.tsx`, sidebar entry on both dashboards) — that page is the intentional policy-transparency surface; the day-to-day Attendance calendar is not. `HrToolService.getShiftSettings()` no longer exists — superseded by `getPolicySummary()`.
- **Note (log #450):** Payroll (`views/Payroll.tsx`) is no longer fake. It used to be `ctc/12` minus a hardcoded flat 7.8% with zero attendance/leave awareness and nothing ever actually stored — "Run Payroll" just flipped a status flag. Now `HrToolService.computePayrollForMonth()`/`runPayroll()` compute real Net Pay = Monthly Gross − (LOP days × Gross÷30), where an LOP day is a working day (not Sunday, not a Holiday-calendar date) with no punch and no approved leave — persisted per-employee-per-month in the new `hr_payroll_entries` table once "Run Payroll" is clicked (frozen; re-clicking recomputes and overwrites). No deductions (PF/ESI/TDS) in this V1 — deliberately deferred, not silently approximated. `HrPayrollRun.month` changed from a human label ("August 2026") to a canonical `YYYY-MM` key — anything reading `state.payrollRun.month` for display needs `monthKeyToLabel()` from `components/admin/hr-tool/utils.tsx`. **Superseded by log #453 below** — the ÷30 rate and month-based cycle described here have both changed.
- **Note (log #451) — important, read before touching Payroll/Directory again:** `hr_employees` (CTC, DOJ, status — used by Payroll and Directory) and `hr_employee_credentials` (Assigning IDs — the actual live hiring flow, has no CTC field, is what attendance is keyed against) are two independent tables that don't auto-sync. The old Onboarding→e-signature pipeline that used to create `hr_employees` rows is dead (unreachable since log #426). Payroll now sources *who appears* from `hr_employee_credentials` (via a `PayrollRosterEntry[]` built in the API `_lib.ts`, not inside `HrToolService`, to keep it decoupled from hr-credentials per its own documented rule), enriching with `hr_employees` data by name-match when it exists; an employee with no `hr_employees` row still shows with `ctc: 0` and a "Set salary" button in Payroll's admin table rather than disappearing. Setting a salary there creates the `hr_employees` row if none exists yet (same defaults as Directory's CSV import). Also split `HrPayrollEntry.presentDays` (was conflating "actually punched in" and "on approved leave" into one number) into separate `presentDays` and `leaveDays` — new `hr_payroll_entries.leave_days` column. **Superseded by log #452 below** — Directory's identical blind spot (flagged here as a known gap) is now fixed.
- **Note (log #452):** Assigning IDs is gone as a separate section — merged into Employee Directory, with hiring now a single flow. `Directory.tsx`'s "+ Send offer letter" (now `HireEmployeeButton.tsx`, replacing the old `DraftOfferLetterButton.tsx`) collects name/designation/team/email/CTC (offer letter fields) AND Employee ID/password/photo/panel-role-link (Assigning IDs fields) in one form — no field asked twice — and on approval creates BOTH the `hr_employee_credentials` login AND the `hr_employees` Directory record together (with the merged offer letter text saved directly on `signedDocs`), employee active immediately (no more fake "awaiting e-signature" limbo, since that stage was never reachable anyway — confirmed dead code, `views/Login.tsx`, deleted nowhere but still orphaned/unused). `hr_employees` gained a real link column, `credential_id` (nullable, soft reference — no FK, since `hr_employees` is saved via whole-table delete/reinsert which a constraint would fight); old rows (Divya Menon, and anyone from before this change) keep `credential_id = NULL` and fall back to name-matching exactly as before, no behavior change for legacy data. New `CredentialFields.tsx` holds the shared Employee-ID/password/panel-role form markup + validation (`nextEmployeeCode`, `generatePassword`, `validateCredentialFields`), reused by both the new hire flow and a new `EditCredentialModal.tsx` — the latter opened from Directory's employee profile (Founder-only, stricter than the rest of the profile modal which HR Head can also see, matching Assigning IDs' original Founder-only gate) for password resets/photo/panel-role changes on an already-hired employee, or to retroactively issue a login for an employee who never had one (e.g. old CSV imports). Directory also gained a **self-healing orphan-credential banner**: any active Employee ID with no matching Directory record (the exact bug class this merge fixes — could still happen from a partial failure mid-hire) shows a one-click "+ Add {name}" that backfills the Directory record from the credential, same pattern as the Payroll fix in #451. Fixed the CSV bulk-import's success message, which falsely claimed "Logins auto-created" — it never did; now says so plainly and points at the new "Issue Employee ID" action. Full file list: added `HireEmployeeButton.tsx`, `CredentialFields.tsx`, `EditCredentialModal.tsx`; deleted `AssigningIds.tsx`, `DraftOfferLetterButton.tsx`; `'assigning-ids'` removed from `HrView`/`VIEW_ACCESS`/`NAV_GROUPS`/`VIEWS`. Deliberately out of scope (confirmed with user before building): reviving Onboarding.tsx's document-upload/e-signature checklist pipeline — that machinery is fully built and already wired, just still has no reachable path feeding it real records; a natural, separate follow-up. Went through full Plan Mode (research → Plan agent → user confirmation on the one real scope question) given the size and sensitivity (creates real login credentials). Hit and fixed a genuine `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint quirk while extracting the panel-admin-lookup effect into `CredentialFields.tsx`: the rule only recognizes the "cancellation-token subscription" exception when the effect's called function takes *only* the `{cancelled}` signal object as its argument — passing other reactive values alongside it (even if the signal is also present) makes it fall back to a flat "no sync setState in effects" violation. Fixed by having the loader function close over the reactive values instead of receiving them as params, matching `Payroll.tsx`'s already-clean `loadPayroll(signal)` shape exactly. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on every touched file. Migration run against live `zox_db` with user confirmation. Not yet tested live.
- **Note (log #454):** The hire form's button label in Employee Directory is "+ Add Employee" now (was "+ Send offer letter" — that label stayed as-is in Onboarding.tsx's own copy of the same button, since the ask was Directory-specific). `CredentialFields.tsx` gained two opt-in props to support this: `showAvatar` (default true; `HireEmployeeButton` passes `false` — no photo field at hire time, still available later via `EditCredentialModal`, which doesn't pass the prop so keeps its default) and `idRowExtra` (a slot next to the Employee ID field for whatever the caller needs in that same row — `HireEmployeeButton` uses it for Date of Joining, so those two now sit side by side instead of DOJ having its own separate row above).
- **Note (log #453) — the live payroll cycle is now 26th–25th, keyed by end month:** `hr_rules.salary_period_from/to = 26/'25'` (was 1/'last'). A cycle like "26 Jul–25 Aug" is stored/displayed under the key `"2026-08"` (the month it *ends* in — matches conventional payroll labeling) — `payrollPeriodRange(monthKey, rules)` in `src/modules/hr-tool/utils/time.ts` resolves that key backward into the actual date range. Whenever "the current cycle" needs resolving client-side, use the new `currentPayrollMonthKey(rules)` from that same file — **not** the old `currentMonthKey()` (still exists in `components/admin/hr-tool/utils.tsx` but is calendar-month-only and will pick the wrong cycle near the wrap boundary). "Run Payroll" now unlocks 5 days before the cycle's real end date (`PayrollPreview.canRun`/`runUnlocksAt`), not after it "ends" — days that haven't happened yet when run early are simply not counted at all (not wrongly treated as LOP). "Present" requires punch in AND out. LOP's per-day rate is ÷ the cycle's actual day count, not a fixed ÷30.

### Public User Registration System
- **DB table:** `public_registrations` (auto-created on first API call via `ensureTable()`)
- **Module:** `src/modules/public-users/` (domain/types.ts + repository/public-users.repository.ts)
- **API routes:** `POST /api/public-auth/register`, `POST /api/public-auth/login`, `POST /api/public-auth/google-verify`
- **Admin API:** `GET /api/admin/registered-users` (requireAdmin, paginated)
- **Frontend:** `src/components/AuthModal.tsx` — bottom slide-up auth banner + floating pill; included in `layout.tsx`
- **Auto-popup behavior:** Logged-out users get an auth banner that slides up from the bottom after scrolling about 900px unless dismissed recently; dismiss applies a 10-minute cooldown instead of suppressing the popup for the entire tab session.
- **Auth storage:** `localStorage` keys `pub_auth_token` + `pub_auth_user`
- **JWT:** `{ pubUserId, email, type: 'public' }`, 30-day expiry, same `JWT_SECRET` as admin
- **Google Sign-In:** GIS library, `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` env var (currently empty — uses fallback button)
- **Protected nav:** REPORTS link (`requiresAuth: true` in config) fires `open-auth-modal` event if not logged in

## Team & Workflow

- **Tony** — Team Lead / Senior Dev. Assigns work and gives final approval/go-ahead.
- **Jarvis** (this agent) — middle layer both ways: receives task requests from Tony and passes them down to Happy; then receives Happy's completed work, does QC (code quality review + testing/typecheck), and reports back up to Tony for approval.
- **Happy** — Junior Dev. Receives tasks (via Jarvis) and implements them.

**Request flow (downward):** Tony asks Jarvis → Jarvis passes the task to Happy.
**Delivery flow (upward):** Happy implements → Jarvis QC's (code quality review + testing/typecheck) → Tony approves → go ahead.

| 130 | 2026-05-25 | Fixed root cause of 404 pages showing INDEX, FOLLOW. (2) two-segment paths returned `{ title: "Post not found" }` when post missing — changed to `notFound()`. Now `notFound()` is called inside `generateMetadata` so Next.js applies `not-found.tsx` metadata (noindex, nofollow) correctly. |

| 136 | 2026-05-25 | Fixed article pages showing NULL robots tag. Root cause: `generateMetadata` in `[...slug]/page.tsx` passed the raw DB string (e.g. `'index,follow'`) directly as `robots` — Next.js requires an object, not a comma-separated string. Added `parseRobots()` helper that converts DB string → `{ index, follow, googleBot: { index, follow } }`. Default changed from `'index,nofollow'` → `index,follow`. Now all article pages correctly emit `<meta name="robots" content="index, follow">`. |

| 138 | 2026-05-25 | Full robots hardcode removal. `posts.utils.ts` (all 3 mappers): fallback changed from `'index,follow'` → `null` — DB value is sole source of truth. `parseRobots()` in `[...slug]/page.tsx`: returns `undefined` when value is null (no override, layout default applies). `admin/posts/edit/[id]/page.tsx` line 122: `'index,nofollow'` → `'index,follow'`. `api/admin/posts/[id]/route.ts` line 231: `'index,nofollow'` → `'index,follow'`. | 141 | 2026-05-26 | Fixed article pages showing INDEX,FOLLOW even when admin set index,nofollow. Root cause: DB stored the 4-char string "null" (not SQL NULL) in the robots column — caused by `String(null).trim()` in the edit API when the form field was missing (null !== undefined triggered, String(null) = "null" which is truthy so the || fallback never fired). `parseRobots("null")` contains neither "noindex" nor "nofollow" → both flags true → INDEX,FOLLOW. Three fixes: (1) Direct DB fix: `UPDATE posts SET robots = 'index,nofollow' WHERE robots = 'null'` — 2 rows fixed including post 609326. (2) Edit API (`/api/admin/posts/[id]`): changed `!== undefined` → `!= null` for robots and contentFollow so null form fields are skipped rather than stored as "null". (3) `hasRobotsColumn()` now auto-creates the column if missing (same pattern as `hasContentFollowColumn`). Also added `revalidatePath(getPostPath(post))` in edit API success so ISR cache is cleared immediately on save. |

## Reports System

- **Feature scope:** Admin-managed reports library with public member-facing access in the dashboard.
- **Admin routes:** `/admin/reports`, `/admin/reports/create`, `/admin/reports/edit/[id]`
- **Public member route:** `/dashboard/reports`
- **Admin APIs:** `src/app/api/admin/reports/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/reports/[id]/route.ts`
- **Public API:** `src/app/api/reports/route.ts`
- **Repository:** `src/modules/reports/repository/reports.repository.ts`

### Reports Data Model

- **Table:** `reports`
- **Columns:** `id`, `title`, `description`, `file_url`, `thumbnail_url`, `file_name`, `file_size`, `mime_type`, `is_active`, `created_at`, `updated_at`
- **Index:** `idx_reports_active_created (is_active, created_at)`
- **Storage:** report files and thumbnails are stored in S3 and referenced by URL.

### Reports Repository Behavior

- `ReportsRepository.ensureTable()` creates the `reports` table if missing.
- The repository also self-heals older production schemas by auto-adding missing columns and the active/created index.
- `file_size` values are normalized to plain numbers before JSON responses to avoid MariaDB `BIGINT` serialization failures.
- Public report listing uses `findActive()` so only active reports are exposed to members.

### Admin Reports Notes

- Admin reports pages use shared admin auth helpers and token fallback handling because some environments strip `Authorization` headers.
- Report upload flow supports direct file upload with S3 presigned URLs.
- Manual fields for file name, file size, and MIME type were removed from admin forms because they are derived automatically from the uploaded file.

### Dashboard Reports Behavior

- `src/app/dashboard/reports/page.tsx` fetches reports dynamically from `/api/reports` using the public auth token from `src/lib/public-auth.ts`.
- The current member page is intentionally simplified compared with earlier iterations.
- Removed from the page UI: summary counters, tag/category pill filters, preview badge overlay on thumbnails, format/pages metadata card, and the remaining reports count text under the search box.
- Current page keeps: hero section, search, report cards, and preview modal.
- The current report cards use a large top thumbnail, oversized editorial title styling, a compact pages/date row, and a bordered right-aligned CTA while still opening the existing preview modal.
- The card proportions were later tightened with a smaller desktop grid, slightly shorter media sizing, and reduced title/meta/button scale for a more compact presentation.
- The cards were then reduced again with a narrower desktop min width, tighter internal spacing, and smaller title/meta/button sizing for an even more compact layout.
- On large screens, the reports grid now uses a fixed four-column layout so four cards can sit in a single row instead of relying on auto-fill behavior.
- Report thumbnails now use contained image rendering inside the media box so the full uploaded image stays visible instead of being cropped.
- Thumbnail boxes now use a soft gradient backdrop derived from each report's color palette so contained images blend into the card more naturally.
- The thumbnail backdrop was later refined into a stronger layered gradient finish with a soft radial highlight so the image area feels more polished and color-led.
- Report card clicks no longer open the old preview modal; they now check `pub_auth_token` and route authenticated users into the dashboard flow while sending unauthenticated users to the public login prompt.

### Current Preview Behavior

- Report cards use `thumbnail_url` as the visual cover when available.
- Opening a report preview uses the uploaded document `file_url` inside a modal when available.
- The preview modal was refined for desktop and mobile sizing so the document uses the available space more cleanly.
- Direct download actions were removed from the member dashboard UI.
- Browser-native PDF toolbar suppression is limited because the preview currently uses the raw document URL in an iframe.

### Operational Notes

- If reports fail with 500s, likely causes previously seen in production were:
  - missing `reports` table columns/index on older schema
  - MariaDB `BIGINT` JSON serialization from `file_size`
  - stripped admin auth headers on admin report pages
- If uploads fail from the browser, S3 bucket CORS must allow the application origin.

### Dashboard Overview Notes

- The old `/dashboard` overview page was removed as the landing surface.
- `/dashboard` now redirects directly to `/dashboard/reports`.
- The earlier dedicated `/api/dashboard/stats` endpoint remains in the codebase, but the dashboard root no longer renders the old overview UI.

### Dashboard Shell Notes

- The shared dashboard sidebar was simplified to a cleaner shell with just the logo, user identity, primary nav items, and logout action.
- The old membership card, secondary nav descriptions, and back-to-site footer action were removed from the sidebar.
- The collapsed desktop rail and mobile drawer were both narrowed so the sidebar behaves better on smaller screens and no longer wastes as much horizontal space.
- In the collapsed desktop state, the brand area now shows only the logo block instead of the full title text.
- The expanded sidebar header was also simplified so the top brand area shows only the logo block, without the StartupNews title text.
- The shared nav was updated so reports is now the primary dashboard destination instead of the removed overview page.

### Dashboard Settings Notes

- `/dashboard/settings` was simplified to remove the extra stat cards, membership benefits, notification preferences, profile tips, quick actions, and danger zone panels.
- The current settings page keeps the profile edit form as the primary surface, with a smaller account snapshot sidebar.
- The profile form area no longer shows the old read-only member/account/status strip, and the copy was updated to avoid membership wording on that page.

### Agent Note

- From this point onward, new project knowledge should be appended at the end of `agent.md` in sections like this, in addition to adding rows to the Interaction Log.

| 251 | 2026-06-11 | Fixed build error: deleted `src/middleware.ts` which conflicted with `src/proxy.ts` (Next.js 16 uses proxy.ts as the middleware file). Fixed draft-post httpStatus inconsistency: `src/modules/posts/utils/posts.utils.ts` and `src/app/api/admin/posts/[id]/route.ts` were returning httpStatus 404 for draft posts, but `/api/posts/robots` and the proxy correctly return 410. Updated all three locations to return 410 for draft posts (matching proxy behavior). |
| 252 | 2026-06-11 | Fixed proxy not intercepting draft posts (returning 404 instead of 410). Root cause: nginx sends X-Forwarded-Proto:https so proxy built internal fetch URLs as https://localhost:3000/api/posts/robots — but Node.js listens on plain HTTP, causing TLS failure caught silently → httpStatus 200 fallback → NextResponse.next() → Next.js 404. Fix: added `toInternalOrigin()` in proxy.ts that rewrites https://localhost to http://localhost for internal fetches. Also fixed build using Turbopack (manifest empty) by adding --webpack flag to npm run build. |

| 259 | 2026-06-11 | Fixed 404 pages missing noindex/nofollow: `not-found.tsx` already had correct robots metadata, but when `notFound()` was called inside `generateMetadata()` in `[...slug]/page.tsx`, some Next.js versions don't reliably propagate `not-found.tsx` metadata into the HTML head. Fix: changed `[...slug]/page.tsx` `generateMetadata` to return `NOT_FOUND_META` (robots: noindex/nofollow) instead of throwing `notFound()` for missing content. The page component still throws `notFound()` to render the 404 page. This ensures noindex/nofollow is in the metadata regardless of which source Next.js uses. |

Fix: added `handleGoogleButtonClick` using `google.accounts.id.prompt()` and wired it to the wrapper div's `onClick`. Added `prompt()` to the Window.google type declaration. | 284 | 2026-06-19 | Fixed "Delete All Articles" not working on /admin/newsletter. Root cause: GET route queried old `newsletter_items` table (dropped in #269) while DELETE route correctly targeted `rss_feed_items` — mismatched tables so delete cleared the wrong source. Fix: rewrote GET to use `rssRepo.findNewsletterItems()` (reads from `rss_feed_items` JOIN `rss_feeds` WHERE feed_for includes 'newsletter'). Now GET and DELETE operate on the same data source. File: `src/app/api/admin/newsletter/route.ts`. |
| 283 | 2026-06-19 | Fixed report page-count not being saved on upload. Root cause: page count was fetched server-side by re-downloading the PDF from S3 (which fails — S3 blocks public GET or times out), and the error was swallowed with `.catch(() => null)`. Fix: added `countPdfPagesFromFile()` (using pdf-lib in browser) to both create and edit pages; count is derived client-side from the File object when the file is selected, then included as `pageCount` in the initial POST/PUT body — no S3 re-fetch needed. Files: `src/app/(admin)/admin/reports/create/page.tsx`, `src/app/(admin)/admin/reports/edit/[id]/page.tsx`. |
| 282 | 2026-06-19 | Fixed bug: RSS feeds marked `feed_for=newsletter` were still creating posts. Root cause: `RssFeedsService.processFeed()` called `postCreator.createPostFromRssItem()` unconditionally. Fix: added `isForWebsite` check — only creates posts when `feed_for` includes `'website'`. Newsletter-only feeds now save the RSS item row but skip post creation entirely. File: `src/modules/rss-feeds/service/rss-feeds.service.ts`. |
| 285 | 2026-06-20 | Fixed wrong images being stored for newsletter RSS items. Root cause: `rss-parser` v3 does NOT parse `media:content` or `media:thumbnail` tags unless explicitly declared as `customFields` — both silent checks in `extractImageUrl` returned undefined, falling back to first `<img>` in content HTML (often an author avatar or logo). Fix: added `customFields.item` with `['media:content', 'media:content']` and `['media:thumbnail', 'media:thumbnail']` in the Parser constructor; rewrote `extractImageUrl` to check `media:content` (array-safe) first, then `media:thumbnail`, then enclosure, then HTML img fallback. File: `src/modules/rss-feeds/service/rss-parser.service.ts`. |


| 286 | 2026-06-20 | Fixed gap after every 4 news items in mobile view on /news and /tech pages. Root cause: CSS rule `.mvp-blog-story-wrap:nth-child(5n+0)` in style.css gives every 5th item a featured full-width layout with extra padding-top:20px, margin:0 on image/text, full-width image, and large font sizes. On mobile the regular side-by-side thumbnail layout couldn't override due to CSS specificity. Fix: added 5n+0 reset blocks in media-queries.css for all mobile breakpoints (max-width: 767px, 659px, 599px, 479px) — resets padding-top:0, restores correct margin-left values for story-out/in, restores thumbnail image size, resets text margins and font sizes to match regular items. File: src/app/styles/media-queries.css. |

| 301 | 2026-06-22 | Fixed report scheduler bugs: (1) `toDbDatetime()` helper converts datetime-local `"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm"` → MariaDB `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"` (T→space + append :00); (2) `isFuture` uses `new Date(rawPublishAt)` consistently; (3) INSERT uses `COALESCE(?, NOW())` so `publish_at` defaults to creation time when not scheduled; (4) UPDATE uses `COALESCE(?, created_at)` so clearing schedule keeps publish_at = created_at; (5) `publishDue` keeps `publish_at` value after activating (used for ordering); (6) PUT route now returns actual error message instead of generic string; (7) removed `min` restriction on datetime picker — admins can set past dates. |

| 312 | 2026-06-25 | Fixed RSS posts showing as "Manual" in admin. Fix: (1) Created `scripts/migrations/add-rss-feed-item-post-id.sql` — adds `post_id` column with `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` and backfills existing rows by title match. (2) Added `deleteFeedItem()` to `RssFeedsRepository`. (3) Refactored `rss-feeds.service.ts` item loop to track `savedItemId` and delete the `rss_feed_items` row on any failure after `saveFeedItem` so the item can be retried next run instead of being permanently orphaned with `post_id = NULL`. |

| 328 | 2026-06-30 | Replaced JS-based sticky sidebar with native CSS `position: sticky; top: 88px` on `.sticky-sidebar-content`. `StickySidebarContent.tsx` is now a simple wrapper with no scroll listeners. Restored `overflow: visible` on `#mvp-side-wrap` (overflow:hidden breaks CSS sticky on ancestors). |
| 327 | 2026-06-30 | Removed `bottom` state from `StickySidebarContent.tsx`. The sidebar now only transitions between `normal` (in-flow) and `fixed` (viewport-pinned at top: 88px). The `bottom: 0` absolute state was the cause of the large blank white gap at the top of the STARTUP EVENTS box when scrolled — it was parking events at the container's bottom edge far below the visible area. With `overflow: hidden` on `#mvp-side-wrap` the events are cleanly clipped when the section ends, so the `bottom` state is no longer needed. |

| 359 | 2026-07-09 | Jarvis QC pass on the uncommitted **Brand Stories** feature (Happy's work, interaction #356, still uncommitted). 8-angle review found: (1) **CONFIRMED bug** — unchecking "Schedule" on the edit page clears `publishAt` client-side but the UPDATE SQL (`publish_at = COALESCE(?, publish_at)`) can't distinguish "no change" from "clear", so the stale future date survives in the DB and the cron scheduler silently auto-publishes the story later anyway despite the admin cancelling the schedule (`src/modules/brand-stories/repository/brand-stories.repository.ts:165`, `edit/[id]/page.tsx:587`). (2) **CONFIRMED** — edit page success banner is dead UI, `setSuccess(true)` is never called before the post-save redirect (`edit/[id]/page.tsx:349`). (3) **PLAUSIBLE**, inherited from Reports — `/dashboard/brand-stories` defaults `sectionFilter` to hard-coded id `1` instead of `null`, so stories can go invisible on the plain landing view if section id 1 doesn't exist (`dashboard/brand-stories/page.tsx:48`, same pattern at `dashboard/reports/page.tsx:48`). (4) Design/maintenance: the entire repository, cron job, and `BRAND_STORIES_ROLES` constant are hand-duplicated from Reports with no shared abstraction — future fixes need manual re-application to both. (5) Efficiency: `ensureTable()` does ~14 sequential migration-check round-trips repeated per route file; admin list `GET` runs `publishDue()` (a write) on every read. No security/auth gaps, no removed guards, no SQL injection — parameterized queries and role checks match the Reports baseline exactly. Findings sent to Tony for review before go-ahead; the scheduling-clear bug (#1) should block approval until fixed. |

Fixed both: `.event-detail-hero` → `aspect-ratio: 1200/630` + `max-height: 630px`; `.event-by-country-card-img` → `aspect-ratio: 1260/630` (matches the recommended image size exactly, minimizing crop for correctly-sized uploads). Verified the source CSS/JSX is correct via `tsc --noEmit`; could not visually re-verify against the local `localhost:3000` preview because that process is a stale `next start` **production** build from before this session's edits (`NODE_ENV=production`, `.next/BUILD_ID` predates the fix) — did not rebuild/restart it since it wasn't started by me and may be in use for something else. Confirmed via `curl` that the compiled CSS bundle still serves the old `16/10`/`1200/560` values, so this needs a rebuild (`npm run build` + restart, or point at the actual dev/staging environment) before it's visually confirmable. |

| 376 | 2026-07-24 | User reported real `partnership-events/import` 500s from `themorningpulse.fyi/admin/partnership-tracker` (plus unrelated 401s on `stats`/`tools`/`footer-copyright`, diagnosed as an expired admin session — log out/in). Batching also incidentally reduces single-request timeout risk for very large imports (route's `maxDuration` is 900s). Contacts page's `Upload Excel` flow was left as the simpler spinner-only loader from #375 (single-file, not batched) since this ask was specifically about partnership-tracker. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| 381 | 2026-07-30 | Follow-up: user said the report **preview modal** itself ("preview") was still slow — different from the grid-load issue fixed in #380. Root-caused two compounding issues specific to `/dashboard/reports`' PDF preview (`src/app/dashboard/reports/page.tsx`): (1) **S3 CORS was hiding range-request support from pdf.js.** Verified via direct `curl -H "Range: bytes=0-1023"` against the S3 object that the bucket (`$S3_BUCKET`, `us-east-1`) *does* support byte-range requests (`Accept-Ranges: bytes`, `206 Partial Content`), but `aws s3api get-bucket-cors` showed the bucket's CORS `ExposeHeaders` only allowed `ETag` through to browser JS — `Accept-Ranges`/`Content-Range` aren't CORS-safelisted response headers, so pdf.js running in the browser couldn't detect streaming support and fell back to downloading the entire PDF (one live report is 13.2MB) before rendering page 1. Fixed by `aws s3api put-bucket-cors`, adding `Accept-Ranges`, `Content-Range`, `Content-Length` to `ExposeHeaders` (kept `AllowedOrigins`/`AllowedMethods`/everything else identical — purely additive) — user explicitly approved this live-infrastructure change first. Verified after: the same range `curl` now shows `Access-Control-Expose-Headers: ETag, Accept-Ranges, Content-Range, Content-Length`. (2) **The viewer rendered every PDF page at once** regardless of scroll position — `Array.from({ length: pdfNumPages })` mounted a full-resolution `<Page>` for all pages immediately on document load. Changed to incremental rendering: new `pdfVisiblePages` state (starts at 2, reset alongside `pdfNumPages` on report change) caps how many `<Page>`s are mounted; a callback-ref-attached `IntersectionObserver` (`pdfSentinelRef`, `rootMargin: '600px 0px'`, rooted on the existing `pdfViewportRef` scroll container) on a 1px sentinel div after the last rendered page bumps `pdfVisiblePages` by 2 as the user scrolls near the bottom, so a 10-page report only pays full-res canvas rendering cost for pages actually being viewed. Rebuilt (`npm run build`) and restarted (`pm2 restart dev-startupgpt`) with user's prior approval from #380 covering the same deploy step; re-verified both the app (200s) and the S3 range/CORS behavior live afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| 382 | 2026-07-30 | User said the PDF preview was "still too much" (slow) even after #381's CORS + incremental-render fixes. Ran a fresh diagnostic: `curl` timing from this box to the S3 object showed a full 13.2MB download in ~0.5s and ~140ms per small range request — i.e. **from this server**, S3 (`us-east-1`) is fast. But the PDF is fetched client-side, in the end user's browser, not from this box — no headless browser available in this environment (checked for `playwright`/`puppeteer`/`chromium`, none installed) to reproduce the user's actual network path, so real end-user latency to `us-east-1` (likely from India, given `TZ=Asia/Kolkata` and the `.fyi` domain) couldn't be measured directly here. Made three more targeted, safe improvements in `src/app/dashboard/reports/page.tsx` on the assumption that per-request latency (not bandwidth) is the real remaining cost for a non-CDN-fronted S3 bucket: (1) Added a module-level `PDF_LOAD_OPTIONS = { rangeChunkSize: 1024 * 1024 }` (1MB vs pdf.js's 64KB default) passed via `<Document options={PDF_LOAD_OPTIONS}>` — fewer, larger range requests trade some extra bytes for far fewer round trips, which wins on high-latency links; kept as a **module-level constant** (not an inline object) since react-pdf treats a new `options`/`file` object identity as "the document changed" and would otherwise silently re-fetch from scratch on every re-render (e.g. every scroll-triggered `pdfVisiblePages` update). (2) The `Document`'s `loading` fallback (shown during initial parse) now renders the report's existing `thumbnailUrl` image (already generated for the grid card) instead of a bare "Loading document…" string, so the modal shows *something* near-instantly instead of blank/text while pdf.js is still fetching/parsing. (3) Added `renderTextLayer={false}` to `<Page>` (joining the pre-existing `renderAnnotationLayer={false}`) — a protected preview (right-click/print/save already blocked) doesn't need selectable text, and skipping text-layer extraction+DOM overlay cuts real per-page render work; also removed the now-fully-unused `react-pdf/dist/Page/TextLayer.css` and `AnnotationLayer.css` imports (both layers are disabled, so neither stylesheet does anything). Rebuilt + `pm2 restart dev-startupgpt` (same pre-approved deploy step from #380/#381), verified 200 afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Flagged to user**: if it's still slow after this, the likely next step is fronting the S3 bucket with a CDN (CloudFront) to cut the physical round-trip distance — that's a bigger infra change than the CORS tweak and would need explicit sign-off + is unverifiable from this box without real end-user network conditions or a headless browser. |

| 387 | 2026-08-07 | User reported images "taking time to upload" in the partnership-tracker Add Event modal (a follow-up to #385's "images not uploading" report — turned out to be slowness, not failure, once clarified). Before changing anything, verified the upload pipeline end-to-end from this box using a real signed JWT (`jsonwebtoken` + live `JWT_SECRET`) against the running `dev-startupgpt` process: `POST /api/admin/presign` returns a valid signed URL, a browser-style `PUT` (Content-Type header only, no checksum header) to that URL succeeds with 200 and the object is retrievable, and an `OPTIONS` preflight simulating `Origin: https://dev.startupgpt.fyi` gets a correct CORS response from the bucket — so presign/auth/S3-policy/CORS were all already fine, confirming the complaint was really about speed, not breakage. Root cause: `src/components/admin/ImageUpload.tsx` (the shared upload component used for posters/banners/creatives across the whole admin panel, not just partnership-tracker) uploaded the original file completely unmodified — a typical phone photo (10-15MB, 4000px+ on the long edge) went over the wire at full size to `us-east-1` with no CDN in front (same underlying latency profile flagged for report PDFs in #380-382) — plus gave zero upload-progress feedback (`fetch()` PUT with a static "Uploading..." label), so even a moderately-slow upload *felt* stuck. Fixed both, client-side only, no new dependencies: (1) `compressImageFile()` — downscales to a 2400px longest-edge cap and re-encodes to JPEG @0.85 quality via `createImageBitmap` + `<canvas>.toBlob()` before upload (skips already-small files <300KB, skips GIF/SVG which a canvas re-encode would break, and always falls back to the original file untouched if compression errors or doesn't actually shrink it). (2) Swapped the S3 `PUT` from `fetch()` (no progress API) to `XMLHttpRequest` with an `xhr.upload.onprogress` handler, so the button now reads "Uploading… 42%" instead of a static string — real feedback, not just a perceived-speed trick. Chose client-side compression over the CDN route flagged in #382 because it needed no infra sign-off and directly cuts the actual bytes transferred (the real lever, not just RTT). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean (one pre-existing unrelated `<img>`-vs-`next/image` lint warning on the same file, not introduced by this change). Per the CSS Cascade Layers spec, unlayered rules always beat layered ones regardless of specificity or source order, so every single about-us Tailwind utility — borders, padding, margins, grid gaps — was silently losing to the legacy reset even though it was correctly present in the served CSS bundle, which is exactly why #411's classes-exist check passed while the page still looked broken. Fix: removed `layer(...)` from both `@import` lines so the CLI emits unlayered output that wins via normal specificity instead (the input file's own header comment already *described* this as deliberate — the actual `@import` lines just contradicted it). | 421 | 2026-08-11 | User asked to make the "auth model" (the public sign-in bottom sheet, `src/components/AuthModal.tsx`) scroll smoothly — its scroll-linked reveal (see #226/#235) was janky because the `onScroll` listener called `setScrollSlideY(...)` on every single scroll event, forcing a full React re-render of the whole component tree (welcome overlay JSX, sheet content, etc.) on every scroll pixel. Fixed by removing the `scrollSlideY` state entirely: added a `sheetRef` on the bottom-sheet `<div>` and a `rafRef`, and the scroll handler now schedules at most one `requestAnimationFrame` per frame (`if (rafRef.current !== null) return;`) which writes `sheetRef.current.style.transform` directly via the DOM, bypassing React's render cycle for the continuous part of the animation. `scrollVisible` state (which only flips twice — reveal start and full-open) is untouched, so React still re-renders normally at those two boundaries; the sheet's inline `style.transform` falls back to `translateY(100%)` on those re-renders, matching the pre-scroll/reset position so the ref-driven value and the declarative value never fight. Also added `willChange: "transform"` while `scrollVisible` for a compositor hint. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the file, no other logic (open/close, Google sign-in, welcome overlay) touched. |

| 422 | 2026-08-11 | Follow-up to #421: user said the auth sheet's reveal still felt "achanak" (sudden/abrupt) when scrolling down, and clarified via `AskUserQuestion` that nothing needs to move on two axes literally — the earlier "x axis and y axis" phrasing just meant "make it smooth," scoped only to the auth modal (not site-wide scrolling, not a specific horizontal-scroll section). Root cause: #421's fix made the transform track raw scroll position pixel-for-pixel via direct DOM writes with no easing, so any large single-frame scroll delta (fast trackpad flick/momentum scroll, or simply crossing the 50px threshold already well past it) caused the sheet to jump straight to that position with no perceptible glide — reads as "sudden" even though technically not re-render-janky anymore. Fix: added `transition: "transform 0.45s cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)"` to the sheet's inline style while `scrollVisible`, so every `requestAnimationFrame`-driven transform write (still applied directly via `sheetRef.current.style.transform`, per #421) now eases into its new value via CSS instead of snapping — including the very first frame, which now glides from the mounted `translateY(100%)` starting point instead of teleporting. This also smooths over uneven scroll-event timing/deltas generally, producing the lagging/catch-up feel typical of "smooth scroll" reveal effects. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Not yet deployed — user explicitly declined the offered `./deploy.sh` rebuild+restart (asked first per the `deploy_pm2_gotcha` memory, since `dev-startupgpt` runs `next start` from a production build and won't pick up source changes without one) and said they'd build/restart themselves. |
| 423 | 2026-08-11 | User reported the auth sheet "is coming not with the scroll of the screen to bottom properly" — read as: the modal doesn't reliably finish opening by the time the user scrolls to the bottom of the page. **Confirmed real root cause**: #421/#422's reveal logic used fixed absolute pixel thresholds (`scrollY` 50px → 600px = full reveal) with no relation to actual page length. On any page whose total scrollable distance (`document.documentElement.scrollHeight - window.innerHeight`) is under ~650px — a real, common case for shorter content pages — `scrollY` can never reach 600, so `progress` never reaches 1 and the sheet gets stuck mid-slide instead of fully opening, no matter how far the user scrolls, including all the way to the literal bottom of the page. Fixed in `applySlide()` (`src/components/AuthModal.tsx`): compute `maxScroll` from the live document height each scroll frame, cap the reveal-end threshold at `Math.min(600, maxScroll)` so the 0→1 progress range always fits within what's actually scrollable, and force `progress = 1` outright once `scrolled >= maxScroll - 1` (i.e. the user has hit the true bottom) as a hard guarantee independent of the pixel-range math. Reveal-start threshold (50px) and the #422 CSS transition easing are unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` clean (a stale `react/jsx-runtime` IDE diagnostic on an unrelated line was environment noise, not a real error — confirmed via a full `tsc --noEmit` pass). Not yet deployed — same pending-manual-deploy state as #422, live site still needs `npm run build` + `pm2 restart dev-startupgpt`. |
| 424 | 2026-08-11 | User said the sheet still felt sudden even after #423, "clearly," while scrolling down. Checked `.next/BUILD_ID` mtime against `AuthModal.tsx`'s mtime and confirmed a rebuild+restart *had* actually landed after #423's fix (build finished 14:06:54 UTC vs. source last edited 14:03:38 UTC, `pm2 describe dev-startupgpt` showed a fresh restart at 14:07:30) — so this wasn't another stale-build false alarm like #411/#416; the reported jump was real and present in the deployed code. **Found the actual root cause**: the moment scroll-tracked `progress` reaches 1, `applySlide()` calls `setScrollVisible(false)` + `setOpen(true)` in the same tick. On the next render, the sheet's `style` object recomputes with `scrollVisible` now false, and since `transform`/`transition` were only ever conditioned on `scrollVisible`, both collapsed back to their "closed" defaults (`transform: undefined`, no `transition`) while `animation: "authSlideUp 0.4s ..."` kicked back in — and that keyframe's own `from` state is hardcoded to `translateY(110%)`. So a sheet that had been smoothly ref-driven up to (visually) ~`translateY(0%)` via #422's transition got yanked back off-screen to 110% for one frame and then replayed the *entrance* keyframe from scratch — a real, confirmed double-jump exactly at the point scroll-tracking "hands off" to the open state, independent of and in addition to #421-#423's fixes (which were all correct for what they addressed, just didn't cover this handoff). Fixed by adding `openedByScroll` state: set `true` right when `progress` hits 1 via scroll (alongside `setOpen(true)`) and reset to `false` in `closeModal()`, `handleLogout()`, and the external `open-auth-modal` event handler (so click-triggered opens still get the normal entrance keyframe). The sheet's style now branches three ways instead of two — while `openedByScroll` is true (and `scrollVisible` is false), `animation` stays `"none"` and `transform`/`transition` are set explicitly to `"translateY(0)"` / the same `0.45s` easing, so the element continues smoothly to its already-correct resting position instead of resetting and replaying `authSlideUp`. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Not yet deployed — same pending-manual-deploy pattern as #422/#423. |

| 437 | 2026-08-14 | Follow-up to #435's Events/Partnership Tracker merge: user reported the new "View: Events →/Regions →/Banners →" links (added in #435) redirected to the login page instead of showing the table, and separately asked to (a) remove "Events" from the admin sidebar entirely now that Partnership Tracker covers it, and (b) show the *actual* Events/Regions/Banners table content inline inside Partnership Tracker, not just link out to a separate page. **Root cause of the login-redirect bug**: the links used `target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"`. `admin-auth.ts`'s `getAdminUser()` (the user object, unlike the token) has no cookie fallback — it's sessionStorage-only — and `rel="noreferrer"` severs the new tab's `window.opener` link, which in turn breaks the browser's same-origin sessionStorage inheritance into that new tab; `(admin)/layout.tsx`'s auth guard calls `getAdminUser()` first and redirects to `/admin/login` immediately when it's null, before ever reaching the cookie-backed token check. Partnership Tracker (`partnership-tracker/page.tsx`) gained an outer 2-way tab bar — "Partnership Tracker" (existing table, unchanged) / "Events, Regions & Banners" (mounts the same `<EventsManagementTabs />`) — via a new `mainTab` state wrapping the existing `{loading ? ... : ...}` block in a `{mainTab === 'tracker' && (...)}` conditional (modals/toast left unconditional, same as before, since they're independently gated by their own open-state and can only be triggered from the tracker tab anyway). The now-redundant "View:" quick-links were removed in favor of the real inline tabs. Removed `{ href: '/admin/events', label: 'Events', icon: EventsIcon }` from `AdminSidebar.tsx`'s `menuItems` — deliberately did **not** touch `ROLE_ALLOWED_PATHS`/the route itself, since `/admin/events` must stay fully reachable (it's still the literal same component now embedded in Partnership Tracker, and still directly linkable). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `eslint` clean (only pre-existing warnings carried over unchanged from the original `events/page.tsx` — confirmed identical before/after the extraction, nothing new introduced). | 437 | 2026-08-14 | Follow-up to #436: user reported More News images still weren't stretching after the `objectFit:"fill"` inline-style change. Root-caused two stacked issues instead of just re-tweaking the same line. (1) `PostImage.tsx` treats its `style` prop as the **wrapper `<span>`'s** style, not the real `<img>`'s (`imageStyle` is the one that reaches `next/image`'s `style` attribute) — so the inline `style={{ objectFit: "fill" }}` passed from `MoreNewsSection.tsx` never touched the actual image element at all, fill or contain. (2) Even if it had, it wouldn't have mattered: `src/app/globals.css`'s "HOMEPAGE THUMBNAILS: LETTERBOX ON WHITE" block (~line 8479) has `.startupnews-mobile-more-news .mvp-blog-story-img img` / `.startupnews-desktop-featured .mvp-blog-story-img img` rules (plus the `:nth-child(5n+0)` variant right after) forcing `object-fit: contain !important` — a stylesheet `!important` rule always beats a non-`!important` inline style regardless of specificity. This CSS, not the component prop, is what actually renders the More News thumbnails site-wide. Fixed at the real source: changed both `object-fit: contain !important` declarations in that block (plain rule ~line 8514 and the 5n+0 override ~line 8538) to `object-fit: fill !important`, matching the same stretch-to-frame pattern the file already uses for the homepage category widgets and hero Trending column immediately below in the same block. Updated the block's header comment to move "More News" out of the "letterbox / not cropped" scope list and note it now stretches instead. Left the inline `imageStyle`/`style` prop question in `MoreNewsSection.tsx`/`PostImage.tsx` alone — CSS is the actual control point here and editing the component further would've been a no-op given the `!important` rules. Not yet deployed. |
| 438 | 2026-08-14 | User asked for a full self-review pass of the Events/Partnership Tracker merge (#435-#437) for remaining edge cases before they test it live — "any edge case handling any issue occurring any error need to fix." Traced through every failure path in `PartnershipEventsService.syncLinkedEvent` by hand and found three real, meaningful gaps, all now fixed in `partnership-events.service.ts`: **(1) Stale `event_id`** — if the linked Event was deleted independently from the new "Events, Regions & Banners" tab, the next Partnership Tracker save would call `eventsService.updateEvent()` on a now-nonexistent id, which throws (`Event with ID X not found`) and would have failed the *entire* partnership-record save just because of an unrelated deletion elsewhere. Now catches that specific failure and falls through to create a fresh linked event instead. **(2) Sync failures could silently duplicate/lose data** — `createEvent`/`updateEvent` had no try/catch around the sync step, so ANY failure while creating/updating the linked Event (DB hiccup, validation edge case) would throw past the already-successful `partnership_events` row insert/update, the API would report failure, the frontend wouldn't refresh its list, and the admin — seeing "failed," unaware the record actually saved — would very plausibly hit Save again and create a duplicate row. Wrapped the whole sync in try/catch; the partnership record save now always succeeds even if the website-listing half can't complete (logged server-side, visible in the UI as "Not listed yet," retried automatically on the next edit+save). **(3) Date only needed for creating a new linked event, not updating one** — the old code required `event_start_date` before ANY sync, meaning clearing that field on an already-published record would silently freeze it (e.g. could never be marked Cancelled again without re-entering a date). Now the date is only required on the create path. Also added, on the frontend (`partnership-tracker/page.tsx`): a validation error if Website Listing Status is set to Published/Cancelled with no Event Start Date filled in (previously this would just silently no-op the sync with no explanation — now caught before save, matching the fixes above); a stronger delete-confirmation warning naming the linked event and its status when one exists, since deleting a Partnership Tracker record does **not** cascade-delete its linked Event (intentional — avoids surprise deletion of live public content, but the previous plain "cannot be undone" confirm gave no hint a live listing would be orphaned); visible error text (not a silent console-swallow) if the new required Region dropdown's data fails to load; and Region/Website Listing Status/Website Event Link added as three new export-only columns in the Excel export, following the exact same established "export-only extra column" pattern already used for Speakers/Social Creatives in the same file. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean. | 440 | 2026-08-14 | User shared a GA4 "Landing page" report screenshot showing `/admin` and `/admin/login` as real landing pages (38 and 26 sessions, 0 key events, unusually long avg engagement 4m39s/2m09s) plus 76 sessions of `(not set)`, and asked why, explicitly requesting no code changes. Root-caused both by reading the tracking setup, not guessing: (1) **`/admin`/`/admin/login` as landing pages** — the GA `gtag.js` snippet in `src/app/layout.tsx` (~line 237-243) is loaded unconditionally in the root layout with no route exclusion, so it fires on every route including admin ones. There is no `middleware.ts` at the project root (only unrelated per-request auth helpers under `src/shared/middleware/`, used inside API routes, not Next.js Edge Middleware) — so `/admin` and `/admin/login` are publicly reachable, plain 200 HTML at the HTTP level. Auth is enforced entirely client-side inside `src/app/(admin)/layout.tsx`'s `checkAuth()`, which only runs after JS/hydration. `public/robots.txt` disallows `/admin` and `(admin)/layout.tsx` sets `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">`, but both only ask well-behaved crawlers not to *index* the page — neither blocks access nor has any effect on GA, which tracks purely from client JS execution regardless of robots.txt. Net effect: any real browser hit on those URLs — genuine staff logging in (explains the long engagement time) or bots/scanners probing for an admin panel (a near-universal, constant background occurrence on any public site) — gets counted as a session landing there. (2) **`(not set)` landing page** — the same `gtag.js`/`gtag-init` `<Script>` tags use `strategy="lazyOnload"`, which Next.js defers until the browser is idle, i.e. after the page has already rendered and, in an App Router site with client-side navigation, potentially after the URL has already changed. If a session's first hit fires late enough to miss a clean `page_location` (fast bounce, bot/prerenderer that doesn't stick around for the idle callback, or a quick client-side redirect like `AdminLayout`'s `router.replace('/admin/login')` for unauthenticated `/admin` visits), GA4 can't populate the landing-page dimension and reports `(not set)`; the accompanying 0 new users on those 76 sessions is consistent with bot/bounce traffic rather than real visitors. No code changes made — diagnosis only, per user's request. |

Fixed by making the distinction real: added `EventNotFoundError` (a typed error class in `src/modules/events/service/events.service.ts`, thrown specifically when `updateEvent` can't find the target row) so `syncLinkedEvent` can tell "genuinely gone, safe to recreate" apart from "some other failure, do NOT recreate" — non-404 failures now just report a warning and leave the existing (stale) link alone instead of duplicating. Also closed the "silent success" gap the bug depended on: `syncLinkedEvent`/`createEvent`/`updateEvent` now return `{ entity, warning? }` instead of a bare entity; both API routes (`route.ts` POST, `[id]/route.ts` PUT) pass `warning` through in the JSON response; the frontend's `saveModal()` now shows that warning via the existing toast (as an error-styled toast, e.g. "The previously linked website event was missing... and has been recreated.") instead of always claiming "Event updated." — so a sync problem is visible to the admin instead of a false-positive success message. Found and fixed a second caller of `partnershipEventsService.createEvent()` that the initial grep missed (`src/modules/event-submission/service/event-submission.service.ts`, the public submit-event flow) once the return-shape change surfaced it as a type error — unwrapped to `{ entity }` there too; that path never actually triggers `syncLinkedEvent` (no `region`/`siteStatus` in its input) so behavior there is unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on every touched file (one pre-existing unrelated `UpdateEventDto` unused-import warning in `events.service.ts`, confirmed via `git diff` to predate this change).
