| 450 | 2026-08-17 | **Payroll module V1** — user asked to design and build a real Payroll system from scratch ("first we create the structure... tell me idea for payroll system"). Research first: HR Management's Payroll page was entirely fake — `grossFor(ctc) = ctc/12` and a hardcoded flat 7.8% "deduction" (not real PF/ESI/TDS), zero attendance/leave awareness despite the page's own copy claiming otherwise, "Run Payroll" just flipped a `{month,status}` flag with no per-employee record ever computed or stored, Download Payslip buttons had no handler — a direct port of the old "Huey" prototype's fake demo math (traced back via git history, `renderPayroll()` in the deleted `script.js`, commit `c9bde0b~1`). Proposed a blueprint and, via `AskUserQuestion`, locked in the V1 business rules with the user: Net Pay = Monthly Gross − (LOP days × Monthly Gross ÷ 30); LOP = a working day (not Sunday, not a Holiday-calendar date) with no punch and no approved leave; **no deductions in V1** (PF/ESI/TDS explicitly deferred, honest placeholder over fake compliance math); "Run Payroll" **freezes** the month (persisted, not recalculated on load) — re-running is just clicking it again (upsert overwrite). Went through full Plan Mode (research → design → approved plan) before writing any code, per the size/financial-sensitivity of the feature. **Schema**: new `hr_payroll_entries` table (one row per employee per month — nothing was ever actually stored before) via `scripts/migrations/add-hr-payroll-entries.sql`, run against live `zox_db`; `hr_payroll_runs` gains nullable `run_at`/`run_by` for an audit trail of who ran a month and when. **Calculation engine** (`HrToolService`): `computePayrollForMonth(monthKey)` (pure, no writes — walks every active, non-exited employee, clips their period to `doj` if hired mid-cycle, **excludes them entirely if they joined after the period ended** rather than wrongly crediting a full month — the one real bug caught during self-review before this was built) and `runPayroll(monthKey, actor)` (computes, refuses if the period hasn't fully elapsed yet since future days can't be judged, then persists via upsert). New `payrollPeriodRange()`/`eachDateInRange()`/`isSunday()` helpers in `src/modules/hr-tool/utils/time.ts`, all built on explicit `Date.UTC(...)`/`getUTC*()` components (never local-time getters on a parsed date string) to avoid a real off-by-one risk — `new Date('2026-08-15')` parses as UTC midnight per spec, and mixing that with local getters could silently shift which weekday/date a server sees depending on its timezone. `payrollPeriodRange` also correctly handles a custom salary cycle that wraps into the next calendar month (e.g. 26th–25th), not just the default 1st-to-last-day case. New repository methods `findLeaveRequestsForEmployeeInRange` (interval-overlap query, since leave has two date columns — `from_date <= ? AND to_date >= ?`, not a simple `BETWEEN`), `upsertPayrollEntry`, `findPayrollEntriesForMonth`. **Month key format switch**: `HrPayrollRun.month` moved from a human label ("August 2026", via the now-removed `currentMonthLabel()`) to a canonical `YYYY-MM` key (matching `monthRange()`'s existing convention) — confirmed via repo-wide grep only two consumers existed outside files already being rebuilt (`Dashboard.tsx`'s stat tile, `Payroll.tsx` itself); added `currentMonthKey()`/`monthKeyToLabel()` in `components/admin/hr-tool/utils.tsx` for the split between canonical storage and display. **API**: new `GET /api/admin/hr-tool/payroll?month=YYYY-MM` (frozen entries if run, else a live preview — same shape either way); `POST /api/admin/hr-tool/payroll-runs` changed from blindly flipping a status flag to actually calling `runPayroll`. **Frontend**: `Payroll.tsx` fully rebuilt — real Employee/Working Days/Present Days/LOP Days/Gross/Net Pay table sourced from its own independent per-month fetch (mirroring `AttendanceWidget.tsx`'s established pattern of not trusting the bulk bootstrap snapshot for something that needs to reflect current server state), "Run Payroll" disabled with an explanatory notice until the period has ended, re-clicking after a run recomputes and overwrites; fake `grossFor`/`deductionFor` and the misleading top-of-file comment removed. `HrToolContext`'s `persistPayrollRun` replaced with `runPayrollForMonth()`. Post-migration, found and deleted one stale `hr_payroll_runs` row (`"August 2026", status: "run"`) left over from someone clicking the old fake button — no `hr_payroll_entries` ever backed it, so it was pure leftover fiction from the placeholder system, confirmed with the user before deleting. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on every touched file. Not yet deployed/tested live — deliberately deferred to a later phase: deductions (PF/ESI/TDS), payslip PDFs, bank/PAN capture, half-day/short-leave partial-day granularity. |

| 453 | 2026-08-18 | **Payroll V2** — a set of real calculation fixes to the V1 engine (#450/#451), all confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` first since this is real-money math. **(1) Payroll cycle is now 26th–25th, not 1st–last day**: `payrollPeriodRange()` rewritten so `monthKey` names the cycle by the month it *ends* in (e.g. "26 Jul–25 Aug" = key `"2026-08"`, the chosen convention — most payroll systems label by payout month) rather than the month it starts in; the wrap-around now goes *backward* into the previous month instead of forward into the next. `hr_rules` singleton row updated live (`salary_period_from=26, salary_period_to='25'`) plus both `DEFAULT_RULES` copies, so this is the actual configured cycle now, not just a supported option. **(2) "Current cycle" resolution had to become period-aware**: the old `currentMonthKey()` (just `today`'s calendar month) would pick the wrong cycle for several days each month once cycles could span two calendar months — added `currentPayrollMonthKey(rules)` to `time.ts` (finds which cycle, by end-month key, today actually falls inside) and switched `Payroll.tsx` and `HrToolContext.tsx`'s initial `payrollRun.month` to use it. **(3) "Run Payroll" unlocks 5 days before the cycle's actual end** (not month-end, and not "must have fully ended") — new `RUN_PAYROLL_UNLOCK_DAYS_BEFORE_END` constant, `runUnlocksAt`/`canRun` added to `PayrollPreview`/`PayrollApiResult`. Running early is now a real, intended use — so days that haven't happened yet (`date > evalTo`, where `evalTo = min(periodEnd, today)`) are simply not judged present/leave/LOP at all rather than wrongly counting as LOP for not having occurred yet; Working Days still always reflects the *full* period regardless of when it's run, since that's a calendar fact, not a time-based one. Re-running after the cycle actually ends (already-supported recompute-and-overwrite) picks up the remaining days for real. **(4) "Present" now requires a full punch — in AND out**, not just a punch-in (previously a punch-in alone counted as present all day). **(5) Per-day LOP rate switched from a fixed ÷30 to ÷ the cycle's actual day count** (`periodDays = eachDateInRange(clippedFrom, to).length`), since 26th–25th cycles run 29-31 days depending on the month; confirmed this is mathematically equivalent to the user's own description of the formula ("present + leave + holiday days, added up, gives the salary") — `Gross − LOP×(Gross/periodDays) ≡ Gross × (paidDays/periodDays)` algebraically, just computed the more numerically-direct way. **(6) Real display bug fixed**: `AttendanceCalendar.tsx` (Payroll's own calendar, Founder view + My Payslips self-view — confirmed in scope, the employee dashboard's separate `AttendanceWidget.tsx` calendar was confirmed out of scope) had zero awareness of Sundays or the Holiday calendar at all — a day with no punch record just showed as generic "Not recorded" (or, if a punch record happened to exist for that date for any reason, whatever raw status it carried, misleadingly implying "present"). Added an explicit Sunday/holiday check (`isSunday()` + `state.orgStructure.holidays`) that now always renders as "Week-off" — taking priority over any punch data — unless an HR manual override says otherwise; fixed both the calendar cell coloring and the day-detail modal's status label, which had the same blind spot independently. **(7) DOJ (date of joining) added as a required field on the "Send Offer Letter" hire form** (`HireEmployeeButton.tsx`) — previously hardcoded to "today," which broke the mid-cycle-join clipping logic for anyone whose actual start date differed from when their offer was processed; kept `signedDate` (when the letter was generated) separate from `doj` (when they actually start) — different concepts, both real. Working Days' Sunday/holiday exclusion in `computePayrollForMonth` itself needed no change — it was already correct (confirmed by hand against the user's own worked example: 31-day cycle, 5 Sundays + 2 holidays → 24 working days), the bug was specifically in the calendar *display*, not the payroll *calculation*. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on every touched file. `hr_rules` data update run against live `zox_db` with user confirmation. Not yet tested live. |
| 454 | 2026-08-18 | Three small UI adjustments to the hire form from #452. Directory's trigger button now reads "+ Add Employee" (was "+ Send offer letter" — left as-is in Onboarding.tsx's own copy of the same button, since the ask was specifically about Employee Directory). Removed the photo-upload field from the hire form itself (still available afterward via Directory → employee profile → Login & Credentials → Edit, which is a separate modal, `EditCredentialModal.tsx`, that kept it). Date of Joining now sits in the same row as Employee ID instead of on its own row above it. Implemented via two new opt-in props on the shared `CredentialFields.tsx` (used by both the hire form and the edit-credentials modal): `showAvatar` (`HireEmployeeButton` passes `false`; `EditCredentialModal` doesn't pass it, so keeps the default `true`) and `idRowExtra` (a slot rendered in a `field-grid-2` alongside the Employee ID field — `HireEmployeeButton` fills it with the Date of Joining input). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean. Not yet deployed. |
